# Backlog

This file is the one list of open backlog rows for Lungfish Genome Explorer. It holds open rows only, each with its effect on results or users, and the section a row sits in is its target phase.
Inside a section the rows run S1, S2, S3, S4, then not rated, and by field within each rating. A row with no rating reads `not rated`.
Every row closed at the Phase 3.4 sort stays readable at `5a0d5f3be:docs/plans/BACKLOG.md` and `5a0d5f3be:docs/plans/BACKLOG-3.2.md`.

## Phase 4

| Id | Effect on results or users | Severity | Field | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| INSPECTORCOLLAPSED-LEAK | The leak observer named `MainSplitSelectionCoordinatorTests/testShowInspectorRequestDropsUnscopedNotificationAndAcceptsItsOwnScope` as leaving `InspectorCollapsed=0` in the `com.apple.dt.xctest.tool` defaults domain. That kept the full tier of `b475214ea` red although its `LungfishAppTests` attempt completed 7990 XCTest cases and failed none, and the quiet gate printed no leak line after GATE FAIL, so the cause stayed hidden. Fixed in 3.3 by FULLFIX (landed `c8a5356e5`, which prints the test state leaks after GATE FAIL and has the test restore the key through `restoreHostDefaultsAfterTest(keys: ["InspectorCollapsed"])` at `MainSplitSelectionCoordinatorTests.swift:279`). A stale `InspectorCollapsed=0` that an earlier run wrote stays in the domain until a person deletes it once (`SCR land/land-35.md:59-62`) | S2 | QA | `SCR reviews/FULL2-QA.md:54-56`, `:68-71`, `:172-175`, `SCR reviews/FULLFIX-QA.md:44` |
| COPY-PLAIN-FOLDER-SIDECAR | Done in 3.2 (6583b7750, 51a71ddf5). Copying a result folder that has no bundle extension to another project fails. `CrossProjectItemCopier.copy` of an imported TaxTriage result throws `missingSourceProvenance`, since `NativeProjectCopyImportService.copy` (`:131`) hands `rehydrateRelocatedImportedCopy` the staged folder, `finalProvenanceRoot` (`GUIImportedProvenanceRehydrator.swift:327`) takes the enclosing project as its root because `isBundleDirectory` (`ProvenanceWriter.swift:556`) accepts any `lungfish*` extension, and the record inside the folder is never looked for. Reproduced at 3a278c4c7 and pinned as a known issue in `testImportedTaxTriageCopiesToAnotherProject`. Other tools' result folders hold the same record and were not run | S2 (proposed) | UX | `Tests/LungfishWorkflowTests/Metagenomics/MetagenomicsImportServiceTests+TaxTriageRehydrate.swift` |
| VIEWER-RETAINS-12S | A 12S viewer stays alive after the person switches away, and it keeps observing. A probe (deleted, never committed) showed a 12S result and then a FASTQ bundle through `MainSplitViewController.displayContent(for:)` and read "controller alive true, parent nil, view in window false", and after the route tests' teardown "window alive false, split alive false, controller alive true". Something outside the window and split graph retains `TwelveSAmpliconResultViewController` (a cycle or a global), and the retainer was not found. Look first at `Sources/LungfishTwelveSUI/TwelveSAmpliconResultViewController.swift:468` and `:610-620`, the closures at `Sources/LungfishApp/Views/MainWindow/MainSplitViewController+ContentDisplay.swift:559-620` and `Sources/LungfishApp/Views/Viewer/ViewerViewController+TwelveS.swift:56`, and take a memgraph of the app after one 12S open and leave. Each 12S result a person opens and leaves stays in memory with its rows for the life of the app, and on every text-size change it saves its old column layout again under the output-name key, which can put an older layout back over the one the person set later. It is why a 12S viewer made by one test kept writing layout keys during later tests, the six leaks that turned the full tier at `673808342` red. LAND-25 (`c7ec1c761`) fixed the test side only and left the app as it was | S3 | ARCH | `SCR lanes/FULL3-LEAKS/FULL3-LEAKS.md:29-37` |
| FIXTURE-SKIP | `testPrimerFixtureDetectsNativeAndReverseComplementOrientations` skips in every tier, in two phases now, on a fixture lookup though the fixture is in the tree (`PrimerTrimFixtureIntegrationTests.swift:23-28`), so primer orientation detection has no running test, and the lookup should follow the resource rule of `Package.swift:207` and fail when the fixture is missing | S3 | BIO | `471167711:docs/plans/2026-10-23-phase-2-14.md:519`, `exit/QA.md:175`, `471167711:docs/plans/2026-10-20-phase-2-13.md:654`, `0c972abf4:docs/plans/2026-10-02-architecture-program.md:266` (BIO), `:267` (QA) |
| EXPORT-DEPTH-FILTERS | The image reads depth with the track's MAPQ (0) and no base-quality filter (`ViewerFigureDepthTrack.swift:72-74`), where the window reads it with `max(minMapQSetting, consensusMinMapQSetting)` and `consensusMinBaseQSetting` (`SequenceViewerView+Alignment.swift:360-361`), both 0 by default (`SequenceViewerView.swift:446,491,494`) and settable to 60 (`ViewerViewController.swift:1243-1246`). The image's max and mean equal the window's at the defaults and differ from a window whose filters were changed, the CLI has no flag for them, and the reads in the image have the same gap. BIO sets the severity | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/EXPORT-IMG-UX.md:126-131` |
| W7-GENOME-MISSING-WORDS | With no genome in the manifest and no MD tag or CIGAR `X`, the image draws no mismatch marks and says nothing, though the viewer draws none either in that case (`ViewerFigureReadDrawer.swift:34`, `:128`, `ReadTrackRenderer+Marks.swift:102-107`) and mapping bundles hold their genome. | EXTRACT-PIN-GAPS | The read-ID pins of each classifier's extraction hold IDs and record counts only (`ClassifierReadIDListSupport.swift:143-168`), so none can see a SEQ or QUAL, orientation or trimming regression. The three NAO-MGS selections avoid the sample with the repeated TSV row, and a pin for it would count 26 records from `samtools fastq` and not 27 from `view` (`ClassifierReadIDListConformanceTests.swift:122-126`, S4) | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/IDPINS.md:95`, `:97`, `:100` |

| FULL1-TRIM-PIN | Done in 3.2 session 2 (LAND-32, `f13d03c97`). `Tests/Fixtures/provenance-record-pins/fastq-trim-record-facts.json` lacked the four read-layout scan keys that READLAYOUT added in `6a72054ff` (`scannedRecords`, `scannedWholeFile`, `pairs`, `unpairedReads`), so `FastqTrimRecordConformanceTests.testTrimRecordKeepsItsFacts` failed in the full tier (5,970 to 7,230 bytes). Test only. Recaptured on BIO's RECAPTURE, byte for byte BIO's file, and the full tier runs again before Stable | S3 | BIO | `SCR lanes/FULL1/FULL1-2.md:71`, `SCR rulings/BIO-TRIM-FACTS.md:6-9` |
| CANCEL-TEST-6S | `SequenceExportPinConformanceTests/testACancelOfAStagedSnapshotStopsTheCommandWithEveryCoreBusy` fails only on elapsed time (6.03 to 6.18 s against its 6.0 s limit) at the end of a 40-minute serial App run, and once alone right after, while its child used 0.62 s of CPU and then slept at 0 percent until SIGKILL. No cause was found, so the test sat in `KNOWN_LOAD_FLAKES` until Phase 3.1 lane FULL1 took it out. In 3.1 the closing serial run failed it 3 of 3 in-test attempts at 6.04 to 6.09 s and it passed alone, because the assertion compares the cancel time with `CLIProcessLauncher.operationGrace`, the instant SIGKILL fires (`SequenceExportPinConformanceTests.swift:402`), so it has under 1% headroom where the child ignores SIGTERM under full CPU load. Lane FULL1 bounded the assertion by `CLIProcessLauncher.operationGrace + ProcessGroupTerminator.killSettleLimit + CLIProcessLauncher.operationDrain` (7.5 s, `Tests/LungfishAppTests/SequenceExportPinConformanceTests.swift:355,413`), so a command that ends by the SIGKILL fallback passes. The row stays open for the product cause, why the child outlives SIGTERM under full CPU load. Leads are whether `CLIProcessLauncher.spec` sets `signalsProcessFirst` (`Sources/LungfishCore/Process/ProcessGroupTerminator.swift:14`) and where `convert`'s compression step waits. The row is S3 since 3.1. | S3 | QA | `events.log:327`, `events.log:329`, `~/lungfish-phase-records/phase-3-1/reviews/LOOP-QA.md:249`, `~/lungfish-phase-records/phase-3-1/events.log:94`, `~/lungfish-phase-records/phase-3-1/reviews/FULL1-QA.md:19-24` |
| TTL-GUARD-REGEX-BLIND | The guard test matches `timeout: TimeInterval = ` followed by a digit, with case, so it could not see `defaultTimeout` (`NativeToolRunner.swift:137`, since reworked by `e62206884`), `mappingProcessTimeout` of 86,400 s (`ONTBarcodeDemuxGenotypingPipeline.swift:169`) or `timeoutSeconds` of 7,200 s (`SavontClusteringPipeline.swift:38`), and it also misses `TimeInterval?` and `Double` defaults, so a new default in those forms passes (`Tests/LungfishWorkflowTests/ToolTimeLimitTests.swift:76`). Check what `e62206884` widened before adding a case | S3 | QA | `SCR reviews/TTL-SWIFT.md:92`, `SCR reviews/TTL-BIO.md:47` |
| OBSERVER-COVERAGE-GAPS | The test-state observer misses four places. It is an `XCTestObservation`, so the Swift Testing step (191 functions in the closing run) runs unobserved (`TestStateObserver.swift:108`). `LungfishPortableTests` and `LungfishExecutorTests` have no observer, which leaves 138 tests unwatched (`Package.swift:150`). Nothing in the gate checks that a bundle's `runner.log` holds the `TestStateObserver:` summary line, and the install pin runs only in the App bundle, so a bundle that lost the observer passes with an empty ledger (`gate_evidence.py:995-1004`, `TestStateObserverTests.swift:12-17`). Nine files outside the App folders set a `LUNGFISH_` variable or the current directory and have never run under it, among them `TaxTriagePipelineProvenanceSourceTests.swift`, `ProcessManagerTests.swift`, `ONTBarcodeDemuxSamtoolsSpawnPinTests.swift`, `NaoMgsNvdImportFixtures.swift`, `CLIEventTranscriptCoverageTests+Managed.swift`, `ManagedToolLeavesTests.swift`, `ExecutablePathSearchTests.swift` and `PrimerTrimGUIIntegrationTests.swift` | S3 | QA | `SCR reviews/LOOP-ARCH.md:135`, `SCR reviews/LOOP-QA.md:251`, `:252`, `:253` |

A count pin would add a source-text assertion, and the ratchet of source-text assertions may not rise | S3 | QA | `SCR/land/land-12.md` Handoff |
| MSA-FIXTURE-PARALLEL-RACE | `MSAPaneFlagFixture(machineFlag:)` (`a3c9cc467`) writes the machine key false on purpose and restores the saved value, so two unit-tier workers that run the routing table and the matrix at once can restore each other's value in the shared defaults domain, a save-write-restore race. It is harmless today, because every MSA view owns its defaults suite, the full tier runs one bundle a process, and a key that is present at the start of a bundle is no leak. The unit tier at `327052394` failed once on it, `MSAPaneFlagFixtureTests/testRestorePutsBackFalse:66`, nil against false, and a diagnostic retry passed 4 of 4. A test-only fix, `f641a7538`, runs each case of the class on a suite of its own | S3 | QA | `events.log:154` (2026-10-29 08:30:51, the manager's reading of LAND-13), `SCR events.log:295`, `:296` |

| DEMUX-EXACT-BARE-NO-FLOOR | Exact Bare Barcode has no chance floor. The run sends the engine to `runExactBareBarcodeDemux` (`DemultiplexingPipeline.swift:113-121`), and only the Cutadapt path calls `requireBarcodesAboveChance` (`:885-890`). The pass looks for each barcode exactly anywhere in the read, in both strands for a custom list or a Fluidigm kit (`DemultiplexingPipeline+ExactBareMatching.swift:288-293`). So on 150-base reads that carry no such barcode, each 8-base barcode matches about 0.44% of reads by chance (2 x 143 / 4^8), about 5% for 12 barcodes, and each becomes a sample file with no words. A person reaches it only by choosing the engine, whose dialog default is Cutadapt (`FASTQOperationDialogState.swift:287`), with a Fluidigm kit (the only built-in kits it offers, `:1096-1097`, `:1650-1659`) or a custom single-index list with no `# kit:` line (`BarcodeKitRegistry+DefinitionLayout.swift:37-47`), on reads that do not carry those barcodes, such as reads whose index is only in their names. A built-in Illumina kit is refused (`DemultiplexingPipeline+Preflight.swift:76-79`). It is old. The engine is in Stable 2026.10.29 (`e4f8f9860`, `DemultiplexingPipeline.swift:111`), when no engine had a floor. BIO re-rated it S1 by effect with rare reach. Lane EXACTBARE-WORDS makes it a Known limit that the app reports in words (`SCOPE-AND-DELIVERY.md:69`). It shows a dialog caption when Exact Bare Barcode is picked, a summary line and an Operations row warning when a run makes sample files, and `chanceCheck: "none"` in the record of every Exact Bare run (`SCR rulings/UX-EXACT-BARE-WORDS.md`). The notes of 2026.11.3 say so. A fix needs a null of its own, since the floor's model is anchored at the read's start. First a BIO ruling on the chance of an exact match anywhere in either strand, measured on whole real reads, then a lane | S1 by effect, rare reach (explicit choice), a Known limit the app reports in words | BIO | `SCR reviews/DEMUXFLOOR-BIO.md:131`, `SCR rulings/BIO-OLDER-DEMUX-ROWS.md` section 2, `SCR rulings/UX-EXACT-BARE-WORDS.md` |
| DEMUX-FLOOR-SATURATED-KIT | When a custom kit's uniform share is 0.5 or more, which means that about half of all random read starts would match one of its barcodes (for example a 384-sample plate of bare barcodes of 4 and 5 bases), the chance floor cannot tell a pool from a file with no barcode at all. Demultiplex Barcodes judges chance as if every base were equally likely, can make sample files out of chance matches and says so in its summary. The head's floor published on all 87 real cuts with QA's 384 kit (form (i) at 4 on 66 of them, an all-reads composition at 2 on 51). No built-in kit is that short, so only a custom kit reaches it. The summary's fallback sentence says the run left too few reads without a barcode to measure its own, and it is said for the other falls to the random-base floor as well (a window over 28 bases, fewer than 100 decoys, three or more errors), where it misleads and the result is unchanged (S3 words). The notes of 2026.11.3 carry the known limit. Since LAND-51 the window shows the floor's own line, as an Operations row warning and a Log line (`FASTQDemultiplexChanceNotice+Floor.swift`, fix `2aba6a166`), and the QA read of the packaged app checks it | S1 by effect, rare reach | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-DEMUX-NULL.md:23`, `SCR reviews/DEMUXNULL-BIO.md:70`, `:97` |
| DEMUX-FLOOR-DECOY-MAX | The decoy maximum is a measured 1-in-1,000 bound for each barcode, not 1e-6. A made 12-base one-error kit on QIAseq mate 2 (SRR36291587 R2) publishes CAACTGCAACAT with 6, 36 and 317 chance reads at 1,500, 10^4 and 85,199 reads, at the base and with the DECOYSCALE fix alike (3 of 147 cuts for that kit, 0 of 147 for the default kit). Held-out decoys beat the floor at a mean 0.034% for a 10-base one-error kit and 0.037% for a 12-base one, against 0.015% and 0.006% at the base, so a 12-barcode one-error kit publishes a false sample in about 0.4% of runs on average. The floor's notes say "rarely" for it, and the notes of 2026.11.3 carry no separate limit line. Amplicon files are its measured worst case. Held-out decoys beat the floor in up to 0.4% of cases there (`SCR rulings/BIO-DEMUX-SPARSE-MARGIN.md` item 9). The dialog's defaults reach it. Cutadapt at error rate 0.15 gives one error to every barcode of 7 to 13 bases. The default Illumina kit carries the bound at a held-out 0.003 to 0.008% a barcode up to 10^5 reads, and 0.018% (worst cut 0.083%) past 200,000 unassigned reads, with 0 of 147 real cuts. The false sample holds about 0.4% of the reads, as a real small sample does, and beats its ceiling 1.2 to 7 times, and nothing marked it before the words (BIO-DECOY-MAX-STABLE). The words landed in LAND-53 (`56444b9df`, red tests `dfaa4467c`), which meets the condition in the severity cell, so the row is now a Known limit the app reports in words | S1 by effect, rare reach, the dialog's default reaches it. | DEMUX-FLOOR-DECOY-SCALE | Fixed in 3.3. On runs with more than 200,000 unassigned reads the floor counted its decoys on the first 200,000 and the barcodes on all of them, so a barcode that is not in the pool could pass as a sample. A made 12-base exact kit on mate 1 of SRR3033154 at 10^6 reads gave CCGATATCGATG 8 chance reads, which a margin of 301 stopped only through a flag. Lane DECOYSCALE (LAND-44, `4d982a7cf`, folds `af3e85a6b`, `600d874ad` and `18071b8d4`) counts every decoy on every unassigned read in one streaming pass, so the rule holds at every size, the default kit publishes in 0 of 147 cuts, and the Known limit line for this row is gone. The pass reads every unassigned read, so it takes longer on runs with millions of them (190 s at 10^7 reads in a debug build, an estimated 40 to 90 s in release) | S1 by effect, rare reach, fixed in LAND-44 | BIO | `SCR land/land-41.md:148-152`, `SCR reviews/DECOYSCALE-BIO.md:86-88`, `SCR land/land-44.md:66-78` |

Demultiplex Barcodes splits the same reads correctly. The defect is old and live in Stable 2026.10.29. LAND-47 Part B (red `bb7860442`, fix `31704c33c`) searches a short-read kit with the barcode anchored at the read's 5' end, as its run does, and the notes carry the line. | NAO-IMPORT-BAM-FAILED-SILENT | Done in 3.2 (ccd8d05d3, 26bf222ff, 70e5d4c31, 6c2935437) in lane NAOBAM, S2 by UX in the older rows meeting (`~/lungfish-phase-records/phase-3-2/meeting/R2-ARCH.md`). A NAO-MGS import in which one sample's BAM cannot be written finishes without a word. The purge of `virus_hits` at `MetagenomicsImportService.swift:1025` does not run for that sample, since it sits under `if !generated.isEmpty` (`:1003`) and a failed sample is never in `generated`, but the sample's rows are lost anyway. The final database is built summary-only (`NaoMgsDatabase+Merge.swift:67-111` holds no `virus_hits`) and the staging root is removed at the end of the import (`MetagenomicsImportService.swift:901`), so the result keeps that sample's summary rows, whose counts are true, with `bam_path` NULL and no reads for it. The only sign is one os_log line (`NaoMgsBamMaterializer.swift:130`), and `createdBAM` stays true when another sample was written (`MetagenomicsImportService.swift:932`, `:956`). Nothing is falsified and the source TSV is untouched, so importing the same file again rebuilds the sample, and the read panels and Extract already say so (`NaoMgsResultViewController+StrandFixNote.swift:37`, `ClassifierExtractionError.swift:111`). The cause is rare, since samtools ships with the app and it takes a full disk or a killed samtools. BIO rates it S3, and it becomes S2 if UX 8 (no words at the moment of failure) is read as covering the import itself. The optional fix is to make the import stop with words, as the BAM copy at `MetagenomicsImportService.swift:767` already does. At `:1001`, after `createdBAM = !generated.isEmpty`, throw a new `MetagenomicsImportError.naoMgsBAMNotWritten(sample:)` when `generated.isEmpty` and `streamResult.hitCount > 0`, and throw the same in an `else` after `:1027` when `managedSamtoolsExecutableURL()` is nil. The `hitCount > 0` test keeps a stage that `minIdentity` emptied out of the throw. The red test is `MetagenomicsImportServiceNaoMgsTests.testImportStopsAndNamesTheSampleWhoseBamCannotBeWritten`, which imports a one-sample NAO-MGS TSV with samtools replaced by a stub that exits 1 and asserts that `importNaoMgs` throws `MetagenomicsImportError.importAborted`, that its description holds the sample name and "alignment file", and that no completed result directory is left. At the base the call returns normally with `createdBAM == false`. The file is near its size baseline, so the guard must fit in the lines it frees. A side effect for other rows is that every result made by the current importer holds no `virus_hits`, so the viewer fallback (`NaoMgsResultViewController.swift:1360-1422`) cannot rebuild a BAM for it, because `generateBam` returns nil at `NaoMgsBamMaterializer.swift:297-300`. Items B3, B5 and B6 of NAO-BAM-MISSING-UX (the rows NAO-MISSING-BAM-EXTRACT-WORDS and NAO-FALLBACK-WRITES-WHILE-DISPLAYED) say "on a result that still holds its reads" and describe only results older than the summary-only merge, and for a newer result the fallback only recreates an empty `bams/` | S2 | UX | `SCR reviews/BACKLOG-BIO.md:15-34`, `SCR reviews/NAO-BAM-STALE-UX.md:119`, `SCR reviews/NAO-BAM-MISSING-UX.md:129` |

| PROJECT-ROOT-LOST-STORE | A project that lost its hidden store and is not named `*.lungfish` is no project under the root test, so the copy faults WF-3 and WF-2b return for it (`ProjectRootTest.swift:29-40`, Ruling 2 of the PROJECT-ROOT review) | S3 | ARCH | `SCR reviews/PROJECT-ROOT-ARCH.md:190-191` |
| NAO-FALLBACK-WRITES-WHILE-DISPLAYED | The missing-BAM fallback writes into a result while it is displayed (`NaoMgsBamMaterializer.swift:54`, `:60`, reached from `NaoMgsResultViewController.swift:1385`). Each card with a missing BAM starts its own fallback and each runs `materializeAll` over every sample, so on a result that still holds its reads several runs write one output path and attempt a markdup pass (`:1381`). The card's spinner goes when the fallback is dispatched, so the cards show no spinner, no words and the grey line until every sample ends (`:1425-1426`). In a read-only project the writes fail and now show words. ARCH and BIO rule on whether the fallback stays a self-healing read before a lane, and a line such as "Rebuilding the alignment file from the stored reads" would say only what is happening | S3 | ARCH | `SCR reviews/NAO-BAM-MISSING-UX.md:114`, `:116`, `:117` |
| ARCHIVE-UNREADABLE-FOLDER | `SafeZipExtractor.swift:115-119` builds the enumerator with no `errorHandler` and returns when it cannot, so a folder that the archive makes unreadable (mode 000) may be skipped silently and a link inside it is never checked. A test that extracts such a folder should confirm it before any fix | S3 | ARCH | `SCR reviews/SYMLINK.md:95` |

One helper in `AlignmentMetadataDatabase.swift` that returns the count and its kind would stop it (`ReferenceBundleViewportController+MappingRows.swift:107-124`, `BundleAlignmentTrackListing.swift:65-75`, `ReadStyleSection+ReadTotals.swift:95-105`) | S3 | ARCH | `SCR reviews/MAPPED-BIO.md:55`, `:91`, `SCR reviews/MAPPED2.md:43`, `:71` |

| BBMERGE-Q50-CAP | With `qin=33 qout=33` bbmerge still saves every score above Q50 as Q50 in the pairs it cannot merge, and `maxcalledquality=93` makes it throw in a worker thread and write no read, so the cap cannot be lifted for bbmerge. A later row could pass `qin=33 maxcalledquality=93` at `MappingCommandBuilder.swift:241`, and the same cap applied at the shipped Preview to every read that did not switch, so it is not a regression | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/BBTOOLS-QIN-BIO.md` ruling 2 and F2, `SCR notes.md:221` |

| BIN-NONE-ROUTES | The app's in-place ingest of a downloaded file (`FASTQIngestionService+CLIRoute.swift:62`, which deletes the original) and its single-file import (`FASTQIngestionService+OperationBegin.swift:161`) have no sheet, so they bin with fiveStep and offer no way to keep every score. The smallest design is one app preference, `Keep every quality score on import`, default off, read at both places and used for the sheet's preselection (`FASTQImportConfigSheet.swift:229`) | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BIN-QIN.md` answer 4, `SCR notes.md:220` |

It is the same question as NVD-UNIQUE-READS-DOUBLE-COUNT, and session 2 measured it on the walk sample. Rotavirus A has 13 unique read pairs, 26 unique mates and no read on two accessions, and BIO ruled that the unit is mates, so 26 is right (`~/lungfish-phase-records/phase-3-1/rulings/BIO-NAOMGS-UNIQUE-READS-UNIT.md:101`). The row stays S3 for one latent defect. No input has met this yet. Its test is `testUniqueReadCountKeepsTwoFragmentsThatShareAMate`, which expects 4 unique reads (2 pairs of 2 mates) and gets 3 today. The release note's Known limit covers it in words | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/ESV.md:90` |
| MAP-POOLED-MIXED-FORMS | A pool that mixes interleaved bundles with R1 and R2 bundles is refused, and a conversion that maps it needs one recorded step per converted input, a fixed mate order and a test on all four mappers. The real obstacle is that a pool carries no read-set plan (`MappingInputResolver.swift:198` returns `readSetPlan: nil` for a pool and `MappingResolvedInputs.readSetPlan` holds one plan, `:25`). | MAP-POOLED-JOIN-RISKS | The pool trusts each input's recorded counts, so an R1 and R2 bundle with unequal files, or an interleaved input with an odd record count, shifts the pairs of every later input, and bowtie2 and BBMap check no names, so the mispairing is silent where a lone bundle stops at the mapper (`MappingPooledInputs.swift:218-227`, `:229-236`). It needs a corrupt input, and the review's later fix compares the recorded R1 and R2 counts of each input before the join, as Merge Into New Bundle does (`READ-PAIRING.md:67`). On minimap2, if the last pair of one input and the first pair of the next share a read name, minimap2 groups the four records as one fragment, and the recorded reason "each keeps its pairs" is false for those four reads (`MappingPooledInputs.swift:224`). | NAO-SYNTH-CIGAR | The synthetic `<length>M` CIGAR of a NAO-MGS BAM places the mates of four reads 1 to 15 bases off, since a soft-clipped or gapped alignment has no real CIGAR (`NaoMgsDatabase+Create.swift:273-277`, `NaoMgsBamMaterializer.swift:486`, `:529`). The TSV's own fragment length differs from the span the CIGARs imply by the same count as the shift. It is not about orientation, and S3 holds until the effect on the duplicate marks is checked. The next edit of the materializer, a file of exactly 800 lines, starts by moving code out | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-NAO-BAM-ORIENT.md:14`, `:76`, `:86`, `:226`, `SCR reviews/NAO-BAM-ORIENT.md:81`, `events.log:36` |
| NAO-INVENTED-SQ-LENGTH | A NAO-MGS BAM gets an invented `@SQ LN:100000` for an accession with no known length (`refLengths[accession] ?? 100000` at `NaoMgsBamMaterializer.swift:305`). It would become S1 if a coverage or depth number divided by that length, and BIO read for that and found that no coverage, depth or percent number of the NAO-MGS view divides by the BAM's `LN`, so it stays S3. The cards divide by the database's lengths, and the current importer writes the BAM at the stage, where every accession with hits already has an `alignment-extent` length, so its header holds that extent and 100000 is reached only for an accession of an older database. The harm is outside the app, since a person who copies `bams/<sample>.bam` out and runs `samtools coverage` or `depth` gets a breadth that is inflated by up to 100% over the reads' extent. No fix is required. If one is wanted later, the header can be written after Phase 5 from the `.fai` lengths with one `samtools reheader` pass per BAM, or the manual can say that the header length is the alignment extent | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/NAO-BAM-ORIENT.md:82`, `SCR reviews/BACKLOG-BIO.md:57-66` |

| BLAST-CLADE-BY-LINEAGE | Done in 3.1 (fixes `effc544a2`, `090c4b9e5`, `e33c48d51` and `bc8d7405f`, with the red tests `7c3dbc7e7`, `c7f70bff7`, `29d3e0a24` and `7cd9368e1`). The five routes now judge at species from the installed taxonomy. What stays open is the half with no taxonomy installed, which is row BLAST-ESVIRITU-FIRST-WORD. Verify with BLAST judges a hit by the claimed tax ID and its name, so a record filed on a lineage node above or beside the claim can count as a different organism. "Rotavirus A" (28875) is a no-rank node under the species 3432193 ("Rotavirus alphagastroenteritidis"), "Human rotavirus A" (10941) sits under 28875, and "Rotavirus G9" (42567) is another child of 28875. A top hit filed at 28875, 3432193 or 42567 therefore counts against a 10941 claim (`BlastTaxonMatching.swift:94-105`, plan at `ViewerViewController+NaoMgs.swift:112`), and TaxTriage, NVD and Kraken2 rows below species have the same gap (`TaxonTree+BlastTaxonomy.swift:23`). No saved input shows it. The walk's 11 saved reads judge 11 supporting, 0 contradicting and Supported on the 10941 row, and 9 of 11 would still read Supported if both tied reads came back topped by a 28875 record. Trigger for S1 is the owner's rerun of the 10941 row showing 2 or more of its 6 to 8 reads topped by a record filed at 28875 or 3432193, which reads Mixed (4 of 6 is 0.667) and blocks the Stable. The ruled fix judges at species level from the installed NCBI Taxonomy `nodes.dmp` (a new `NCBITaxonomyLineage`, at most 5,000 IDs, the name rule when no taxonomy is installed) and starts the Kraken2 clade at the report tree's species ancestor, and the ruling advises taking it into 3.1. The orange "conflicting organisms" count on that row (`BlastTaxonMatching.swift:119-128`) has the same cause and does not feed the badge | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-CLADE.md:118-133`, `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-CLADE.md:332-345`, `SCR reviews/BLAST-SCOPE-BIO.md:143-153` |

| NVD-EXTRACT-TAXON-GROUPS | In NVD's By Taxon grouping, two or more grouping rows selected alone reach the multi-selection bar, which enables Extract Reads. The selector builder skips `.taxonGroup`, so the selectors are empty, the guard returns and the button does nothing and says nothing, and a mix of grouping rows and contigs reads "N rows selected" while the dialog counts only contigs (`NvdResultViewController.swift:1636`, `:2750-2753`). To see it, open By Taxon, Shift-click two taxon rows and press Extract Reads. Later fix is to grey Extract with the R4 words when no contig is selected | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/ADOPT-BIO.md:118-121` |
| ESV-BAM-PATH-NOT-CHECKED | The EsViritu words for a missing alignment file (R10) fire only when the database records no BAM path. A recorded path is kept with no file check (older databases keep absolute paths), so a moved BAM goes on to the extraction (`ViewerViewController+EsViritu.swift:270`) and fails with the extraction service's words through `drawer.failure` (`:172-175`), which were not read. Nothing is sent to NCBI either way, and those words may not say so (`EsVirituResultViewController.swift:596-603`, `ViewerViewController+EsViritu.swift:90-93`) | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/ADOPT-BIO.md:122-125` |
| NAOMGS-READ-NAMES-TRY | `try? db.fetchReadNames` reads a thrown query as an empty set, so the selector drops its read-name allowlist and the extraction goes by accession and tax ID, which can take other taxa's reads aligned to the same accessions. An empty list is also the designed answer for merged results, so the dialog cannot tell the two apart (`NaoMgsResultViewController.swift:1871`) | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/ADOPT-BIO.md:135-137` |
| NAOMGS-QUERY-STEP-ENDS-ON-ERROR | `while sqlite3_step(stmt) == SQLITE_ROW` ends on any non-row result, so a statement that fails to run reads as no rows or as the rows so far (`NaoMgsDatabase+Queries.swift:399`, `:78`). The viewer then sends fewer reads to BLAST or takes the BAM route with no words. It was seen once, in the first form of a test where `fetchVirusHitsForBLAST` returned no rows after the table was dropped under an open handle, and a real trigger is rare | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/ADOPT-BIO.md:138-140`, `SCR lanes/ADOPT/ADOPT.md:288` |
| BLAST-READ-SELECTION-UNDECLARED | "X/Y verified" does not say how the Y reads were chosen, so a subset by position or by best match can read as a taxon-wide check. EsViritu draws the first 5,000 reads in BAM order (`ViewerViewController+EsViritu.swift:256`, `:278`), the NAO-MGS BAM route takes the first unique names for the top accessions (`NaoMgsResultViewController.swift:2330`, `:2404`) and its database route takes the lowest edit distance first (`NaoMgsDatabase+Queries.swift:385`) | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/ADOPT-BIO.md:145-149` |
| BLAST-SAMPLE-BY-FRAGMENT | BAM routes sample mates, and a chosen longest mate's sibling leaves the pool (`BlastService+Subsampling.swift:82-85`). After BLAST-MATES the words show it. Trigger is a large row whose fragment count misleads a reader | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-MATES.md:137` |
| BLAST-NAOMGS-ONE-MATE | The NAO-MGS fallback sends the first record of a name, whichever mate that is. One read is sent and one judged, so it is honest. Trigger is a saved NAO-MGS result with inconclusive reads whose other mate matches. Saved records so far read 18 of 18 verified | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-MATES.md:138` |
| BLAST-TAXONOMY-BUILD-IN-RECORD | Neither `search.taxonomy` nor `lineage` in a saved BLAST record names the taxonomy build. The accepted IDs are in the record and the CLI command, so the verdict can be reproduced without the build, but a reader cannot say which build chose them. Add it at the next schema change. For the same reason a hit filed under a node newer than the local dump falls to the name rule (the dump on the walk Mac is dated 2026-10-09). BLAST-ESVIRITU-TABLE-IDENTITY joins this row | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-WALK.md:22`, `:382`, `SCR walk/walk-log.md:172` |
| BLAST-ESVIRITU-IMPORTED-NAMES | An imported EsViritu result, with `databasePath` `/imported` (`Sources/LungfishWorkflow/Metagenomics/MetagenomicsImportService.swift:105`), on a Mac without the EsViritu database keeps names only, so it is judged by names. The `Name` route could serve if `Name` and `species` must agree on one species. Trigger is an owner or user report on an imported EsViritu result | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-WALK.md:22`, `:385`, `SCR walk/walk-log.md:172` |
| BLAST-CATCHALL-SPECIES | Some species-rank nodes are catch-alls such as `Polyomavirus sp.` and `Hepacivirus sp.`, and a few EsViritu tax IDs lie under them. If such a node has unrelated children, the species-level rule accepts them all, the false Supported direction. For EsViritu rows it is measured and closed (79 roots, 152 accessions, 0.5%, at most 2 descendants each). It stays open for NAO-MGS, NVD and TaxTriage claims, which nobody has measured. Trigger is a saved supporting read whose top hit shares only such a root | S3 | BIO | `SCR rulings/BIO-BLAST-WALK.md:22`, `:388`, `SCR walk/walk-log.md:172`, `SCR reviews/BLAST-ESVIRITU-SPECIES-BIO.md:84`, `:158` |
| BLAST-NAME-PHRASE-LEAK | A hit outside the clade IDs counts as clade when its organism holds the row's name or a path name as a whole phrase (`Sources/LungfishCore/Services/Blast/BlastTaxonMatching.swift:96-99`), so records of another NCBI species can support a row (710 of 7,190 EsViritu rows, 28% of accessions). The same step serves NAO-MGS, TaxTriage and NVD claims, unmeasured there. Trigger is a saved supporting read whose top hit sits in another species at 90% or more through the name alone | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/BLAST-ESVIRITU-SPECIES-BIO.md:149` |
| BLAST-ESVIRITU-TABLE-IDENTITY | The saved `search.taxonomy` holds IDs and names but not the EsViritu table's version or path (`Sources/LungfishIO/Formats/EsViritu/ViralDetection+BlastVerification.swift:38-47`), so an import judged through another version's table cannot be told from a native run. | EXPORT-NOHIT-MATE-WORD | In the exported BLAST table the no-hit mate of a supporting or contradicting fragment carries the fragment's Taxon Check word beside blank hit columns and a Verdict of `unverified` (`Sources/LungfishWorkflow/ViewerExport/BlastVerificationTableExport.swift:65-67`, `:69-87`), so a reader who counts rows and not read IDs overcounts. The notes line should say the word is per read ID | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/EXPORT-CHECK-BIO.md:112` |
| EXPORT-ERROR-REASON-MISSING | The `errorMessage` of an `.error` read (set at `BlastService+Verdicts.swift:109`) is not written (`BlastVerificationTableExport.swift:62-87`), so the exported file reads `error` with no reason | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/EXPORT-CHECK-BIO.md:113` |
| EXPORT-HIDDEN-VERDICT-COLUMN | The contig and sequence drawers hide the status column (`Sources/LungfishKit/BlastResultsDrawerTab.swift:1385`, `:1391`), yet the export writes a Verdict column for them that the person never saw | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/EXPORT-CHECK-BIO.md:114` |
| EXPORT-NVD-NO-TAXID-CLADE | An NVD hit with no integer adjusted tax ID has tax ID 0 and an empty clade (`NvdBlastHit+BlastVerification.swift:30-32`, `:63`), so the export says `no taxon queried` while the verification drawer shows counts judged by name against `classificationName`. A clade of its name, as TaxTriage builds at `TaxTriageOrganism+BlastVerification.swift:28-30`, would fix it but changes verdicts | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/EXPORT-CHECK-BIO.md:177` |
| EXPORT-LEGACY-ARCHIVE-NO-TAXON | A legacy archive saved with no request and tax ID 0 (an old EsViritu verification, if any exist) reads `no taxon queried` in the export though it was judged by name (`BlastVerificationTableExport.swift:132-135`) | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/EXPORT-CHECK-BIO.md:178` |
| TAXTRIAGE-RUN-AGAIN-INDEX-FALLBACK | Run Again's index falls back to the held index (`TaxTriageResultViewController+BlastRestore.swift:65`), so a sample with a BAM entry and no index entry could pair its BAM with another sample's index once a sample segment is chosen. Not shown. The batch handler resolves it from the BAM (`TaxTriageResultViewController.swift:2043-2044`) | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/REOPEN-BIO.md:172` |
| BLAST-RESTORE-SCAN-DECODES-ALL | Each click decodes every record in the folder before it tests the match (`Sources/LungfishWorkflow/Metagenomics/BlastVerificationArchive+SavedSearches.swift`, `newestSubmission`), and in 12S every `updateActionBar()` starts such a scan, including each filter keystroke (`TwelveSAmpliconResultViewController.swift:1516`). The scan runs off the main actor and the next click cancels it, so it only delays, but a project with many large searches means disk reads while arrowing. Idea is to test the lineage before decoding the result. UX rated it S4 and BIO S3 | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/REOPEN-UX.md:101`, `SCR reviews/REOPEN2-UX.md:104`, `SCR reviews/REOPEN2-BIO.md:142` |

| TWELVES-CLI-MISSING-TABLE-ZERO | The loader still reads a missing unresolved table as no sequences (`Sources/LungfishIO/Bundles/TwelveSAmpliconResultBundle.swift:158-161`), so `lungfish-cli fastq 12s-export-unresolved` on a damaged bundle gives zero sequences with no word that the table is missing, though the viewer now says so (lane STALE12S, Fix C). The lane rated it S4. A silent zero is a number a CLI user may trust, so BIO rates it S3, and it is rare since it needs a damaged bundle | S3 | BIO | `SCR reviews/STALE12S-BIO.md:98`, `SCR lanes/STALE12S/STALE12S.md:57` |
| TWELVES-IMPORT-REPLACES-RUN-RECORD | A metadata import replaces the bundle's run record with its own (`Sources/LungfishApp/Services/SampleMetadataBundleImportService.swift:58-154`, which writes the caller's envelope over `.lungfish-provenance.json`), and a metadata edit appends a step to that record and writes it again (`persist`, `:262-374`). After an import the 12S run's argv, times and tool versions are no longer in the file, so anything that reads the bundle record as the 12S run's would find the import. `verifyTwelveSAmpliconBundleProvenance` runs only after an app run, so it is not hit. | TWELVES-STALE-SCREEN | After a 12S result is replaced on disk while it is open, the screen keeps the old numbers and only the actions stop. Export refuses in words and BLAST refuses when its query is not the rows on screen (lane STALE12S), and a person can still read numbers off the old screen until they select another item in the sidebar and then the result again. The release notes say so in a Limit. The ruling rated the stale pane alone S3 and said a later row can make the viewer notice a replace by itself. A verified top hit in such a record counts as supporting for a claim below the name, for example a bat betacoronavirus 1 record for a Human coronavirus OC43 claim. Trigger is a saved supporting read whose top hit sits in a record of another species and only in a path name. A narrowing needs the context to know which IDs the dump holds, so it is not for now. | BLAST-LINEAGE-MERGED-CLAIM | A claim on a merged tax ID reads nil from `speciesClade` and keeps `[taxId]`, for example SARS-CoV-1's 227859, merged into 694009 (`NCBITaxonomyLineage.swift:34`), and a hit filed under a node newer than the dump falls to the name rule. | BLAST-TOP-SCORE-VARIES | Narrowed in 3.2 (9b3f47634, d7fdad850). A search now asks NCBI for 500 records when a claim holds tax IDs, a shared tie shows its first clade record first, and the record keeps the tie summary and the database sequence count. Why a list of 5 and a list of 500 kept different best scores at NCBI is not explained. One case, for BIO-BLAST-TIES. On rota7 read `2129:11905:11145`, a list of 5 had a best bit score of 261.5 and a list of 500 a best of 256.0. A wider list should not lose the best record, so either the list size changes what NCBI keeps or a run can miss it. If real, a read's verdict can change with the list size | S3 (proposed) | BIO | `SCR blast-debug/sends/b01-rota7-lge`, `t2-rota7-500-a` |
| REFERENCE-GENOME-NO-STAMP | A replaced FASTA of the same layout and length reads the new bases under the old annotations, variants and alignments, and no reader can see that without a stamp. BIO rates it below the short read, since LGE then returns exactly what the file holds at the index's coordinates and the person made the change. It rests on reading and on one measured case (V3 in the ruling), and was not run at equal length. An mtime check would falsely refuse a copied project, so a stamp means a checksum in the manifest | S3 | BIO | `~/lungfish-phase-records/phase-3-2/rulings/BIO-FASTA-REACH.md` Q2 |
| BLAST-KRAKEN2-NT | Planned 3.2 (O130). Kraken2's Verify with BLAST searches `nt` (`TaxonomyViewController.swift:1422`, with the request's default at `BlastVerificationRequest.swift:116`), and every other route searches `core_nt` (`BlastSubmitRoute.swift:79`), so the same reads can get another verdict by route. Row BLAST-S4-GROUP's phrase that all five routes search `core_nt` is wrong for this one | S3 (proposed) | BIO | `0c972abf4:docs/plans/2026-11-03-phase-3-2.md` |
| BLAST-OTHER-DATABASE | Planned 3.2, as the rulings set (O130). A person cannot search a database of their own, so reads with private data must leave the Mac for NCBI or go unchecked, and every search depends on NCBI's queue. `blast submit --database` takes only an NCBI database name, the app offers no choice, and `BlastServiceEndpoints.swift` names no other host by design. `blastn` is managed in the `full-length-mhc-genotyping` pack. A narrow database repeats the false Supported of BIO-NAOMGS-BLAST-SCOPE, so BIO rules first | S3 (proposed) | BIO | `0c972abf4:docs/plans/2026-11-03-phase-3-2.md` |

The export makes the read track from `ViewerFigureReadTrack.Options()`, which has no masking field and no other consensus setting (`ViewerGraphicExport+Figure.swift:221-223`, `ViewerFigureReadTrack.swift:65-83`), and the fact holds the track's id, kind, display mode and height only (`ViewerGraphicExport+Figure.swift:33-39`, `:255-262`). The code comments cite a project rule against `runModal` that no rule file holds, and `FailurePresenter.swift:104-108` already falls back to a modal alert. | DEMUX-STAGED-INPUT | The app stages a demultiplex's inputs (`FASTQOperationExecutionService.swift:331-334`, `:683-707`) although the CLI resolves a virtual bundle itself (`FastqCommand.swift:268-284`, `:408`, `:550`). A demultiplex of a subset or other derived FASTQ, or of a bundle of several files such as an ONT import, so keeps `-o '<derived>'` in its Command box (`FASTQOperationRowCommand.swift:147-151`), the Copy CLI Command does not reproduce the run, and the run's record names a staged copy that the run deletes. The fix is upstream. Stop staging for demultiplex and hand the CLI the bundle, which it keeps as the lineage source. The row must not spell the staged input as the request's one input, since that makes it differ from its record. The owner of the execution service confirms first that the CLI route gives the same reads and pairing (`FASTQOperationExecutionService.swift:398-407`). BIO asks for a probe before the row closes. If pairs are split the row is S1, and if only the lineage link is lost it is S2. The probe is not measured.
